03Inventory, Allocation, and ReplenishmentThe national total cannot promise store pickup+

Your goal

Translate inventory states into an accurate location-specific availability message.

This lesson in the book, page 22 ↗

A Read the situation

The national total cannot promise store pickup

At 09:00, SKU L42 has 100 units physically on hand at the central warehouse: 60 committed to existing orders, ten held for quality review, and 30 available for new sales. Twenty additional units are incoming and are not included in on-hand stock. Store East has no units. The website incorrectly uses the warehouse total to display pickup availability at Store East. Inventory controller Miro and digital trader Anika must correct the promise and check delivery eligibility before offering a warehouse fulfillment route.

Miro: Inventory controller / Anika: Digital trader

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. How many warehouse units are available for new sales?

Answer and explanation

A. 30

One hundred on-hand units less sixty committed and ten held leaves thirty available.

2. How many incoming units are included in the on-hand total?

Answer and explanation

D. None

The brief explicitly separates twenty incoming units from the one hundred physically on hand.

3. What is established for Store East?

Answer and explanation

B. It has no units.

Store East has zero units, so warehouse stock cannot establish local pickup readiness.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

On hand is not the same as available. Here the 100 warehouse units comprise 60 committed, ten held, and 30 available. The 20 incoming units are additional expected stock, not stock already received.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
on-hand inventory
Stock physically held at the stated location.reconcile on-hand inventory
available inventory
Stock eligible for new sales under the stated rules.confirm available inventory
committed inventory
Stock already allocated to existing demand.protect committed inventory
unavailable inventory
Held stock not currently eligible for sale.classify unavailable inventory
incoming inventory
Stock expected but not yet received into the location.track incoming inventory
quality hold
A restriction pending the applicable product-quality review.maintain a quality hold
allocation
Assignment of stock to a demand source or destination.revise the allocation
replenishment
Restocking to support future sales or demand.schedule replenishment
available to promise
Quantity that can be committed under defined fulfillment rules.calculate available to promise
stockout
Lack of the required sellable stock at a location.report a store stockout
location-level availability
Sellable stock information for a particular site.verify location-level availability
inventory feed
Data supplied to another system about stock quantities or states.correct the inventory feed
safety stock
Stock held as a buffer against uncertainty.review safety stock
reorder point
The stock position that triggers replenishment under a policy.set a reorder point
lead time
Elapsed time between defined order and supply milestones.verify replenishment lead time
transfer order
A record authorizing movement between inventory locations.create a transfer order
cycle count
A targeted physical count of selected inventory.perform a cycle count
inventory adjustment
A recorded change to the system's stock quantity.authorize an inventory adjustment
overselling
Accepting demand beyond what can be fulfilled as promised.prevent overselling
backorder
Unfilled demand awaiting stock under the applicable arrangement.manage a backorder
fulfillment eligibility
Whether an item and location may serve a specified order.check fulfillment eligibility
stock reservation
Holding specified stock for a defined demand.confirm a stock reservation
inventory snapshot
Quantities and states captured at a particular time.timestamp the inventory snapshot
channel allocation
Assignment of inventory to particular sales channels.review channel allocation

Warehouse availability does not establish store pickup or a delivery promise. Check location, item, eligibility, reservation, and timing under the actual process. An inventory snapshot can become stale as orders and stock movements occur.

C Notice the language

Ask which stock number the team means

Merchandising, stores, and digital teams may all say available while referring to different records. Repeat the location and status instead of assuming agreement. This makes a correction collaborative and prevents a national total from becoming an unsupported local promise.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Identify the item
We are checking SKU L42.
Timestamp the quantity
This is the inventory position at 09:00.
Separate physical and sellable stock
One hundred units are on hand, but only thirty are available.
Protect existing orders
Sixty units are already committed.
Respect the hold
Ten units remain under quality review.
Keep incoming stock separate
Twenty units are incoming, not yet received.
State the local position
Store East currently has no units.
Correct the promise
The warehouse total must not drive a ready-for-pickup message here.
Check the route
Can this warehouse fulfill this customer's delivery address?
Avoid an assumed reservation
Availability alone does not mean this order has reserved stock.
Request a fresh check
Refresh the quantity before making the offer.
Name the system issue
The feed is using the wrong location and inventory state.
Keep the hold intact
Do not release held units just to cover the website promise.
Limit a transfer claim
A proposed transfer is not a received replenishment.
Coordinate the correction
Update the customer-facing message and the underlying feed.
Close with a verified option
Offer a delivery route only after eligibility is confirmed.
6 language and precision notes
At 09:00
Fixes the time scope of a changing quantity.
Already committed
Protects demand that has an existing allocation.
Not yet received
Distinguishes incoming units from physically held stock.
Currently
Signals a present position, not a permanent stockout.
Only after
Makes the offer conditional on the relevant check.
Underlying feed
Names the data source, not merely the visible message.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which website claim is supported now?

Answer and explanation

B. Store East has no stock for pickup.

The local store has no units; the warehouse total does not change that fact.

2. Which arithmetic matches warehouse availability?

Answer and explanation

C. 100 minus 60 minus 10 equals 30.

Both existing commitments and the quality hold must be removed from the on-hand total.

3. Which step requires a separate check?

Answer and explanation

B. Whether the warehouse can fulfill the customer's delivery route

The brief leaves delivery eligibility unconfirmed despite the warehouse having thirty available units.

4. Which wording overstates a proposed transfer?

Answer and explanation

B. The replenishment has arrived at Store East.

A proposed stock movement does not establish physical receipt at the receiving store.

Check explanations in the book, page 89 ↗

E Conversations

The national total cannot promise store pickup

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Miro: Inventory controller / Anika: Digital trader

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • committed inventory
  • incoming inventory
  • stock reservation
  • quality hold
  • inventory snapshot
  • transfer order
  • on-hand inventory
  • fulfillment eligibility
  • inventory feed
  • location-level availability
  1. Anika
    East is getting pickup requests for L42, but the manager says the shelf is empty. The page shows one hundred units. Can we trace where that number came from?
  2. Miro
    Start with the 1 at nine this morning. The hundred units belong to the central warehouse, not Store East. The page is drawing the wrong conclusion from that total.
  3. Anika
    Even at the warehouse, can we sell all one hundred to new customers?
  4. Miro
    No. That is 2. Sixty are committed to existing orders, and ten are held for quality review. Only thirty remain available for new sales.
  5. Anika
    We also have twenty on the way. Someone added them to the website quantity.
  6. Miro
    Keep 3 separate. Those twenty have not been received. Adding them to current sellable stock would create a promise we cannot support from this snapshot.
  7. Anika
    Could we use the ten held units until the next shipment arrives?
  8. Miro
    The 4 remains in place. We cannot release them merely because the page overpromised. The correction must respect the actual stock states rather than make them fit the message.
  9. Anika
    And the sixty already assigned cannot be offered a second time.
  10. Miro
    Correct. Protect 5. We should not solve one customer promise by quietly taking stock from another order. The available balance here is thirty, not ninety or one hundred.
  11. Anika
    I will remove the Store East pickup claim. Could we offer delivery from the warehouse instead?
  12. Miro
    First check 6. Available stock does not prove that this warehouse serves the customer's address or that the order meets the route's conditions.
  13. Anika
    If delivery is possible, can I say we have put one aside? I have not created a reservation yet.
  14. Miro
    Only after the actual 7 is confirmed. Other orders may arrive after our snapshot. Refresh the quantity before offering a specific commitment.
  15. Anika
    That handles this customer. We also need to stop the same error on other product pages.
  16. Miro
    Review the 8. It needs the correct location and state, not just a national physical total. Otherwise the visible correction will be overwritten by the next update.
  17. Anika
    The store manager has asked for a warehouse transfer. Can we mark the store replenished?
  18. Miro
    Not yet. A 9 is different from receipt. Check approval and timing, then update the store position when the relevant events actually occur.
  19. Anika
    I will show no pickup stock at East, check the warehouse delivery route, and raise the feed correction.
  20. Miro
    Good. Keep 10 visible in the handoff. That gives stores, digital trading, and customer service one accurate basis for the next conversation.

Answers and explanations
  1. inventory snapshot Turn 2. Inventory snapshot fixes the quantity and status at a particular time; these records describe the position at nine.
  2. on-hand inventory Turn 4. On-hand inventory includes all physically held units in this case, including commitments and units on quality hold.
  3. incoming inventory Turn 6. Incoming inventory is expected stock not yet received; it must not be counted as already held at this warehouse.
  4. quality hold Turn 8. Quality hold restricts the ten units pending review; commercial demand does not itself authorize their release.
  5. committed inventory Turn 10. Committed inventory is allocated to existing demand and cannot be treated as unassigned stock for another sale.
  6. fulfillment eligibility Turn 12. Fulfillment eligibility establishes whether this item and warehouse may serve the proposed order and delivery route.
  7. stock reservation Turn 14. Stock reservation assigns stock to a particular demand; a general available balance does not establish that assignment.
  8. inventory feed Turn 16. Inventory feed supplies stock information to the website; its location and state mapping must support the displayed promise.
  9. transfer order Turn 18. Transfer order records an authorized inter-location movement; it does not prove the receiving store already has the goods.
  10. location-level availability Turn 20. Location-level availability ties sellable quantities to a specific site, preventing warehouse inventory from implying local pickup readiness.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Anika: East is getting pickup requests for L42, but the manager says the shelf is empty. The page shows one hundred units. Can we trace where that number came from?
  2. Miro: Start with the inventory snapshot at nine this morning. The hundred units belong to the central warehouse, not Store East. The page is drawing the wrong conclusion from that total.
  3. Anika: Even at the warehouse, can we sell all one hundred to new customers?
  4. Miro: No. That is on-hand inventory. Sixty are committed to existing orders, and ten are held for quality review. Only thirty remain available for new sales.
  5. Anika: We also have twenty on the way. Someone added them to the website quantity.
  6. Miro: Keep incoming inventory separate. Those twenty have not been received. Adding them to current sellable stock would create a promise we cannot support from this snapshot.
  7. Anika: Could we use the ten held units until the next shipment arrives?
  8. Miro: The quality hold remains in place. We cannot release them merely because the page overpromised. The correction must respect the actual stock states rather than make them fit the message.
  9. Anika: And the sixty already assigned cannot be offered a second time.
  10. Miro: Correct. Protect committed inventory. We should not solve one customer promise by quietly taking stock from another order. The available balance here is thirty, not ninety or one hundred.
  11. Anika: I will remove the Store East pickup claim. Could we offer delivery from the warehouse instead?
  12. Miro: First check fulfillment eligibility. Available stock does not prove that this warehouse serves the customer's address or that the order meets the route's conditions.
  13. Anika: If delivery is possible, can I say we have put one aside? I have not created a reservation yet.
  14. Miro: Only after the actual stock reservation is confirmed. Other orders may arrive after our snapshot. Refresh the quantity before offering a specific commitment.
  15. Anika: That handles this customer. We also need to stop the same error on other product pages.
  16. Miro: Review the inventory feed. It needs the correct location and state, not just a national physical total. Otherwise the visible correction will be overwritten by the next update.
  17. Anika: The store manager has asked for a warehouse transfer. Can we mark the store replenished?
  18. Miro: Not yet. A transfer order is different from receipt. Check approval and timing, then update the store position when the relevant events actually occur.
  19. Anika: I will show no pickup stock at East, check the warehouse delivery route, and raise the feed correction.
  20. Miro: Good. Keep location-level availability visible in the handoff. That gives stores, digital trading, and customer service one accurate basis for the next conversation.
Dialogue in the book, page 28 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

National stock does not mean store availability

Fictional inventory update: a product is available in one warehouse but unavailable in several stores; the website displays a single national total.

  1. Store operations manager: The website says the product is available, but several stores are reporting a stockout.
  2. Inventory planner: Stock is available in one warehouse. The national total doesn't establish stock at each store.
  3. Store operations manager: Has replenishment to those stores been completed, or is it still a proposed allocation?
  4. Inventory planner: Store replenishment isn't confirmed in the information we have. We need the location-level status.
  5. Store operations manager: Then we shouldn't tell customers they can collect locally based on the national number.
  6. Inventory planner: Correct. Available warehouse stock, allocated stock, and delivered store stock are different statuses.
  7. Store operations manager: I'll ask the replenishment team for verified allocations and delivery status for the affected stores.
  8. Inventory planner: Good. Keep any arrival estimates separate from confirmed receipt and usable stock.
  9. Store operations manager: The update will identify warehouse availability and the unresolved store replenishment instead of saying supply is fixed.
  10. Inventory planner: That gives a clear current position without turning aggregate inventory into a local pickup promise.
Allocated is not yet dispatched

Separate fictional replenishment case: 24 units are allocated to Store 6, but the warehouse dispatch record remains open.

  1. Store manager: Twenty-four units are allocated to our store. Can I tell the team the shipment has left?
  2. Replenishment coordinator: Not yet. Allocation is confirmed, but the warehouse dispatch record is still open.
  3. Store manager: So the completed action reserves the planned quantity, while physical dispatch remains pending.
  4. Replenishment coordinator: That's the current case status. We should check the actual record before announcing transit or arrival.
  5. Store manager: Could we display those twenty-four units as shelf stock to reduce online stockout messages?
  6. Replenishment coordinator: No. Allocation doesn't establish store receipt or inventory available for sale from your shelves.
  7. Store manager: I'll keep the store quantity unchanged and ask the warehouse for a dispatch update.
  8. Replenishment coordinator: Good. If they provide an estimate, label it as an estimate rather than confirmed delivery.
  9. Store manager: Our briefing will say twenty-four allocated, dispatch pending, and no verified store-arrival time yet.
  10. Replenishment coordinator: That preserves progress without letting a planning status become a promise to customers.
The stock count is complete, the discrepancy is not

Separate fictional inventory case: a store count finds 17 units while the system shows 20; a discrepancy review is open and no adjustment is approved.

  1. Inventory control lead: The physical count is complete: seventeen units counted against twenty shown in the system.
  2. Store replenishment assistant: Can I mark the inventory-accuracy task complete because the counting team has finished its work?
  3. Inventory control lead: The count is finished, but the three-unit discrepancy remains under review. Those are different tasks.
  4. Store replenishment assistant: Should I assume theft explains the difference and adjust the number immediately in the system?
  5. Inventory control lead: Neither the cause nor an adjustment is established here. Follow the authorized discrepancy-review process.
  6. Store replenishment assistant: I'll report the two quantities and keep the review status separate from the completed count.
  7. Inventory control lead: Good. Replenishment decisions need the verified inventory status, not an unexplained correction made for convenience.
  8. Store replenishment assistant: I'll ask the review owner for the next update and flag the unresolved quantity to planning.
  9. Inventory control lead: Please don't claim improved inventory turn from this count difference; that measure requires other information.
  10. Store replenishment assistant: Understood. The update will focus on counted quantity, system quantity, and the outstanding reconciliation.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

The national total cannot promise store pickup
  1. Read turns 1-10; separate on-hand, committed, held, and incoming quantities.
  2. Swap roles for turns 11-20; check delivery eligibility before offering warehouse stock.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Read a second inventory snapshot

At Warehouse North, 80 units are on hand: 45 committed, five held, and the rest available. Twelve more are incoming. Store South has zero units.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Trader: "The available warehouse quantity is ___."

Answer and explanation

C. 30

Eighty on hand minus forty-five committed and five held leaves thirty available.

2. Controller: "The held quantity is ___."

Answer and explanation

A. five

The briefing explicitly identifies five units as held rather than available for sale.

3. Trader: "The incoming quantity is ___."

Answer and explanation

A. twelve

Twelve units are expected separately and are not part of on-hand stock.

4. Controller: "Store South's current quantity is ___."

Answer and explanation

C. zero

The warehouse quantities do not change Store South's explicitly stated zero stock.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Lesson 03: Inventory, Allocation, and Replenishment
Original fictional case: At 09:00, SKU L42 has 100 units physically on hand at the central warehouse: 60 committed to existing orders, ten held for quality review, and 30 available for new sales. Twenty additional units are incoming and are not included in on-hand stock. Store East has no units. The website incorrectly uses the warehouse total to display pickup availability at Store East. Inventory controller Miro and digital trader Anika must correct the promise and check delivery eligibility before offering a warehouse fulfillment route.
Language workshop: The national total cannot promise store pickup
Communication goal: Translate inventory states into an accurate location-specific availability message.
Language guidance: On hand is not the same as available. Here the 100 warehouse units comprise 60 committed, ten held, and 30 available. The 20 incoming units are additional expected stock, not stock already received.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): We are checking SKU L42. / This is the inventory position at 09:00. / One hundred units are on hand, but only thirty are available. / Sixty units are already committed. / Ten units remain under quality review. / Twenty units are incoming, not yet received. / Store East currently has no units. / The warehouse total must not drive a ready-for-pickup message here. / Can this warehouse fulfill this customer's delivery address? / Availability alone does not mean this order has reserved stock. / Refresh the quantity before making the offer. / The feed is using the wrong location and inventory state. / Do not release held units just to cover the website promise. / A proposed transfer is not a received replenishment. / Update the customer-facing message and the underlying feed. / Offer a delivery route only after eligibility is confirmed.
Vocabulary: on-hand inventory: Stock physically held at the stated location. / available inventory: Stock eligible for new sales under the stated rules. / committed inventory: Stock already allocated to existing demand. / unavailable inventory: Held stock not currently eligible for sale. / incoming inventory: Stock expected but not yet received into the location. / quality hold: A restriction pending the applicable product-quality review. / allocation: Assignment of stock to a demand source or destination. / replenishment: Restocking to support future sales or demand. / available to promise: Quantity that can be committed under defined fulfillment rules. / stockout: Lack of the required sellable stock at a location. / location-level availability: Sellable stock information for a particular site. / inventory feed: Data supplied to another system about stock quantities or states. / safety stock: Stock held as a buffer against uncertainty. / reorder point: The stock position that triggers replenishment under a policy. / lead time: Elapsed time between defined order and supply milestones. / transfer order: A record authorizing movement between inventory locations. / cycle count: A targeted physical count of selected inventory. / inventory adjustment: A recorded change to the system's stock quantity. / overselling: Accepting demand beyond what can be fulfilled as promised. / backorder: Unfilled demand awaiting stock under the applicable arrangement. / fulfillment eligibility: Whether an item and location may serve a specified order. / stock reservation: Holding specified stock for a defined demand. / inventory snapshot: Quantities and states captured at a particular time. / channel allocation: Assignment of inventory to particular sales channels.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10; separate on-hand, committed, held, and incoming quantities. Swap roles for turns 11-20; check delivery eligibility before offering warehouse stock. Complete the transfer without adding incoming units to available stock.
Partner: Miro: Inventory controller / Anika: Digital trader
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: At Warehouse North, 80 units are on hand: 45 committed, five held, and the rest available. Twelve more are incoming. Store South has zero units.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

04Fulfillment, Shipping, and ReturnsAn expired estimate needs an honest next step+

Your goal

Acknowledge a missed delivery estimate, distinguish shipment from arrival, and explain a concrete follow-up.

This lesson in the book, page 31 ↗

A Read the situation

An expired estimate needs an honest next step

Order R81 was physically handed to the carrier on 4 May. The customer was given an estimated arrival date of 6 May. It is now 7 May, and the carrier has not confirmed a new arrival time. Customer Laila needs the item for an event. Agent Tomas can open a carrier inquiry and give a status update at 14:00 today. No replacement dispatch, refund, or revised arrival is authorized in the supplied record. The team must check applicable customer rights and service remedies rather than invent a universal rule.

Laila: Customer / Tomas: Customer service agent

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What is confirmed?

Answer and explanation

A. The carrier physically received the order on 4 May.

Physical handoff on 4 May is established; delivery and remedies remain unconfirmed.

2. What does 14:00 today represent?

Answer and explanation

C. A status update

The agent promises a communication update, not any unconfirmed shipment or financial outcome.

3. What is unknown?

Answer and explanation

D. The revised arrival time

The carrier has not confirmed a new arrival time in the supplied record.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

A shipping label alone does not prove carrier possession. This case does establish physical handoff. Shipment on 4 May still does not establish delivery on 6 May or any revised arrival time.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
fulfillment
The process of preparing and completing an order's supply.track order fulfillment
dispatch
Sending goods from the seller's fulfillment operation.confirm dispatch
carrier handoff
Physical transfer of a parcel to the transport provider.verify carrier handoff
estimated delivery date
A projected arrival date rather than an unconditional guarantee.revise an estimated delivery date
tracking event
A recorded milestone in a shipment's movement.check the latest tracking event
carrier inquiry
A request for the transport provider to investigate a shipment.open a carrier inquiry
proof of delivery
A record supporting that delivery occurred.request proof of delivery
delivery exception
An event interrupting the expected delivery process.investigate a delivery exception
last mile
The final transport stage to the recipient.coordinate last-mile delivery
delivery window
A stated interval during which arrival is expected or scheduled.confirm the delivery window
service level
The defined standard or terms of a delivery service.verify the service level
consignment
Goods sent together under a transport arrangement.identify the consignment
tracking number
An identifier used to follow a shipment.provide the tracking number
split shipment
An order sent in separate deliveries.explain a split shipment
partial fulfillment
Supply of only part of an order.record partial fulfillment
delivery attempt
An effort to deliver at the specified address.verify a delivery attempt
return to sender
Routing a parcel back to the sending party.investigate return to sender
replacement order
An order created to supply a substitute for an earlier item.authorize a replacement order
refund authorization
Approval to return the relevant payment.confirm refund authorization
service recovery
Actions addressing a failure in the customer experience.coordinate service recovery
return authorization
Permission or reference for a return under a process.issue a return authorization
reverse logistics
Movement and handling of goods back from customers.manage reverse logistics
return inspection
Examination of returned goods under the applicable process.complete a return inspection
restocking fee
A charge associated with a return where applicable and lawful.explain a restocking fee

An update promise must not become an arrival promise. Applicable shipment-delay obligations, in-transit remedies, and return rights require the actual transaction and jurisdiction; do not treat every delay as the same legal event.

C Notice the language

Own the communication without inventing control

Customers need an accountable seller contact even when a carrier holds the parcel. Avoid blaming another company or offering vague reassurance. State the verified event, the unanswered question, and a follow-up you can actually deliver.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Acknowledge the missed estimate
The estimated date has passed, and you still do not have the order.
Recognize the impact
I understand that you need the item for an event.
State the verified milestone
The carrier physically received the parcel on 4 May.
Avoid an unsupported date
I do not have a confirmed revised arrival time.
Take the next action
I can open an inquiry with the carrier now.
Own the follow-up
I will update you today at 14:00.
Separate contact and arrival
That is the update time, not a delivery guarantee.
Check the latest event
I will confirm what the tracking record actually shows.
Avoid blame
I will remain your contact while we investigate.
Limit the remedy claim
No replacement has been authorized in this record.
Check available options
I will review the applicable service remedies and customer rights.
Confirm before promising
I need confirmation before giving a new arrival window.
Avoid false certainty
The absence of a new scan does not prove the parcel is lost.
Keep the order reference
I will record the inquiry against R81.
Provide an honest update
I will contact you even if the carrier has not answered.
Close with accountability
The next message will state the latest position and outstanding action.
6 language and precision notes
Physically received
Distinguishes possession from a label being generated.
Estimated
Marks the stated arrival as a forecast, without erasing relevant obligations.
I do not have
Identifies the present information gap directly.
Even if
Keeps the communication commitment valid while an answer is pending.
No ... authorized
States the record's limits without deciding all possible remedies.
Remain your contact
Maintains customer-facing ownership across another party's investigation.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which response is both honest and useful?

Answer and explanation

A. The carrier has no confirmed new arrival; I will investigate and update you at 14:00.

The supported response combines the real uncertainty with an action and a communication commitment.

2. Which event proves shipment in this case?

Answer and explanation

D. Physical carrier handoff on 4 May

The briefing establishes actual carrier possession, not merely preparation or an estimated future event.

3. Which promise is not supported?

Answer and explanation

A. A replacement will arrive tomorrow.

Neither replacement authorization nor a replacement arrival date is supplied.

4. What should a pending-carrier update do?

Answer and explanation

D. State that the reply is pending and identify the continuing action.

An honest update preserves the commitment without fabricating an answer or excluding applicable rights.

E Conversations

An expired estimate needs an honest next step

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Laila: Customer / Tomas: Customer service agent

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • carrier handoff
  • estimated delivery date
  • refund authorization
  • replacement order
  • service recovery
  • carrier inquiry
  • delivery exception
  • delivery window
  • dispatch
  • tracking event
  1. Laila
    My order was due yesterday. I need it for an event, and the tracking has stopped changing. Can you tell me whether the carrier actually has it?
  2. Tomas
    I am sorry the 1 has passed without the order reaching you. I can see R81, and I will check the actual shipment events rather than repeat the old date.
  3. Laila
    Does the record show that you sent it, or only that someone printed a label?
  4. Tomas
    It confirms 2 on the fourth of May. That is stronger than a label-created message, but it does not tell us when the parcel will reach your address.
  5. Laila
    The delivery was meant to be the sixth. Can you promise tomorrow instead?
  6. Tomas
    I cannot confirm a new 3 yet. The carrier has not supplied one. I would rather give you a verified update than another date that might cause you to make unreliable plans.
  7. Laila
    What can you do now? I do not want to be told simply to keep refreshing the website.
  8. Tomas
    I can open a 4 and remain your contact. I will ask for the current location and the latest delivery position, then update you today at fourteen hundred.
  9. Laila
    Do you mean the order will arrive by two, or that you will contact me then?
  10. Tomas
    I mean I will contact you. The latest 5 may help explain the delay, but the update appointment is not an arrival promise. I will make that distinction clear in the note.
  11. Laila
    Could you send another one now so that I have a backup?
  12. Tomas
    A 6 has not been authorized. I can review the available remedies under the actual order and service terms, including any applicable customer rights, before confirming an option.
  13. Laila
    I would also like to know whether a refund is possible if the item cannot meet my need.
  14. Tomas
    I will check that rather than invent a 7. The answer depends on the relevant facts and rights. I will record your event requirement so the review addresses the problem you actually face.
  15. Laila
    The missing scans worry me. Does that mean the parcel is lost?
  16. Tomas
    Not by itself. A 8 or a delayed update needs investigation. I do not have evidence that establishes loss, and I will not label the shipment delivered without support either.
  17. Laila
    Please still call at two if you have not heard back. I need time to arrange something else for the event.
  18. Tomas
    I will. The 9 note will include your concern, the inquiry, and the outstanding answer. You should not have to start the explanation again with another agent.
  19. Laila
    Thank you. I need a clear position so I can decide what to do about the event.
  20. Tomas
    Understood. We will keep the verified 10 information separate from arrival and remedy decisions. My next contact is today at two, with the actual position even if some questions remain open.

Answers and explanations
  1. estimated delivery date Turn 2. Estimated delivery date names the projected arrival that has passed; it is not proof that delivery occurred.
  2. carrier handoff Turn 4. Carrier handoff means the parcel was physically transferred to the transport provider, which is established for 4 May.
  3. delivery window Turn 6. Delivery window identifies an expected or scheduled arrival interval; no revised interval is confirmed here.
  4. carrier inquiry Turn 8. Carrier inquiry is the concrete request for shipment investigation that the agent is authorized to initiate.
  5. tracking event Turn 10. Tracking event is a recorded shipment milestone; checking it supports an update without guaranteeing a future arrival.
  6. replacement order Turn 12. Replacement order supplies another item, but this record contains no authorization for the agent to promise one.
  7. refund authorization Turn 14. Refund authorization is approval for returning payment; the agent must not imply an approval that the record lacks.
  8. delivery exception Turn 16. Delivery exception identifies an interruption to the expected process; it requires investigation rather than an invented lost-parcel finding.
  9. service recovery Turn 18. Service recovery concerns addressing the customer experience after the delay, including accountable follow-up and checking available remedies.
  10. dispatch Turn 20. Dispatch concerns sending the goods; it must remain separate from the still-unconfirmed arrival at the customer's address.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Laila: My order was due yesterday. I need it for an event, and the tracking has stopped changing. Can you tell me whether the carrier actually has it?
  2. Tomas: I am sorry the estimated delivery date has passed without the order reaching you. I can see R81, and I will check the actual shipment events rather than repeat the old date.
  3. Laila: Does the record show that you sent it, or only that someone printed a label?
  4. Tomas: It confirms carrier handoff on the fourth of May. That is stronger than a label-created message, but it does not tell us when the parcel will reach your address.
  5. Laila: The delivery was meant to be the sixth. Can you promise tomorrow instead?
  6. Tomas: I cannot confirm a new delivery window yet. The carrier has not supplied one. I would rather give you a verified update than another date that might cause you to make unreliable plans.
  7. Laila: What can you do now? I do not want to be told simply to keep refreshing the website.
  8. Tomas: I can open a carrier inquiry and remain your contact. I will ask for the current location and the latest delivery position, then update you today at fourteen hundred.
  9. Laila: Do you mean the order will arrive by two, or that you will contact me then?
  10. Tomas: I mean I will contact you. The latest tracking event may help explain the delay, but the update appointment is not an arrival promise. I will make that distinction clear in the note.
  11. Laila: Could you send another one now so that I have a backup?
  12. Tomas: A replacement order has not been authorized. I can review the available remedies under the actual order and service terms, including any applicable customer rights, before confirming an option.
  13. Laila: I would also like to know whether a refund is possible if the item cannot meet my need.
  14. Tomas: I will check that rather than invent a refund authorization. The answer depends on the relevant facts and rights. I will record your event requirement so the review addresses the problem you actually face.
  15. Laila: The missing scans worry me. Does that mean the parcel is lost?
  16. Tomas: Not by itself. A delivery exception or a delayed update needs investigation. I do not have evidence that establishes loss, and I will not label the shipment delivered without support either.
  17. Laila: Please still call at two if you have not heard back. I need time to arrange something else for the event.
  18. Tomas: I will. The service recovery note will include your concern, the inquiry, and the outstanding answer. You should not have to start the explanation again with another agent.
  19. Laila: Thank you. I need a clear position so I can decide what to do about the event.
  20. Tomas: Understood. We will keep the verified dispatch information separate from arrival and remedy decisions. My next contact is today at two, with the actual position even if some questions remain open.
Dialogue in the book, page 37 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

The delivery estimate was not a guarantee

Fictional fulfillment case: a delivery estimate has passed; the carrier has not confirmed a new arrival time. A retail service lead speaks with a business buyer.

  1. Business buyer: Your message said the order would arrive yesterday, but the carrier hasn't delivered it.
  2. Retail service lead: I'm sorry our wording made an estimate sound guaranteed. No revised arrival time is confirmed.
  3. Business buyer: We scheduled staff to receive it. Can you now promise delivery tomorrow instead?
  4. Retail service lead: I can't make that promise without carrier confirmation. I'll request the current shipment status.
  5. Business buyer: Then please explain what your fulfillment team has completed and what remains outside that confirmation.
  6. Retail service lead: I'll verify those steps in the record rather than assume a dispatched message proves delivery.
  7. Business buyer: Does the service-level agreement automatically entitle us to a particular payment for this delay?
  8. Retail service lead: We need the applicable agreement and authorized review; I shouldn't invent compensation terms here.
  9. Business buyer: Please correct the timing message and tell me who will follow up with the carrier.
  10. Retail service lead: I'll own that follow-up and communicate verified updates without replacing the missed estimate with another guarantee.
A return label was mistaken for a refund

Separate fictional returns rehearsal: a supervisor practices a customer-facing correction with a returns coordinator. A return label is issued, but an email calls the refund complete; receipt, review, and refund status are not established.

  1. Customer support supervisor: Our email says refund complete, but the record only confirms that a return label was issued.
  2. Returns coordinator: That wording is wrong. A label enables the return process; it doesn't establish a completed refund.
  3. Customer support supervisor: I'm sorry our email incorrectly said your refund was complete. Only your return label is confirmed; your refund status remains unverified.
  4. Returns coordinator: That wording acknowledges our error and separates the confirmed label from the unverified refund without promising payment.
  5. Customer support supervisor: Could I promise the refund will be completed as soon as the customer uses the label?
  6. Returns coordinator: No. Use the actual policy and case process rather than inventing an automatic outcome or deadline.
  7. Customer support supervisor: I'll ask the returns team for the verified next step and keep unknown timing explicit.
  8. Returns coordinator: Please also correct the internal contact note so other agents don't repeat the completed-refund claim.
  9. Customer support supervisor: I'll keep the correction separate from the outstanding returns checks.
  10. Returns coordinator: That gives the customer an honest process explanation without treating a document as proof money has moved.
A split shipment was described as complete

Separate fictional order case: one of two parcels is delivered; the second has no confirmed arrival time. The retailer's message says the whole order arrived.

  1. Corporate purchasing assistant: Your message says the order arrived, but we received only one of the two parcels.
  2. Fulfillment support agent: I'm sorry. The message described the whole order as delivered when only one parcel is confirmed.
  3. Corporate purchasing assistant: Can you explain the second parcel's status without repeating the same overall delivered label?
  4. Fulfillment support agent: Its arrival time isn't confirmed. I'll request the carrier's verified status for that parcel specifically.
  5. Corporate purchasing assistant: Should we assume the second parcel is lost because it didn't arrive with the first?
  6. Fulfillment support agent: No. The different arrival status doesn't establish loss or its cause from the information available.
  7. Corporate purchasing assistant: Please keep the parcel references separate so our receiving team knows which update concerns which box.
  8. Fulfillment support agent: I will, and I'll correct the order-level message rather than blame your team for reporting the mismatch.
  9. Corporate purchasing assistant: I'll tell receiving that one parcel is delivered and the other remains unconfirmed.
  10. Fulfillment support agent: I'll follow up on the second parcel and avoid promising a delivery date the carrier hasn't verified.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

An expired estimate needs an honest next step
  1. Read turns 1-10; distinguish carrier handoff, estimated arrival, and the update time.
  2. Swap roles for turns 11-20; offer a review of real remedies without guessing the outcome.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Give a second delayed-order update

Order V32 was handed to the carrier on 12 June. Its estimated arrival was 14 June. Today is 15 June. A new arrival time is unconfirmed; the agent will update the customer at 16:00.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Agent: "Carrier handoff occurred on ___."

Answer and explanation

C. 12 June

Twelve June is the confirmed physical handoff date, not the estimated arrival.

2. Customer: "The original estimated arrival was ___."

Answer and explanation

C. 14 June

Fourteen June is the supplied estimate that has already passed.

3. Agent: "The revised arrival time remains ___."

Answer and explanation

A. unconfirmed

No new arrival time is established by the facts in this exchange.

4. Customer: "The next status update is at ___."

Answer and explanation

D. 16:00

Sixteen hundred is the contact commitment and must not become a delivery guarantee.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary

3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Lesson 04: Fulfillment, Shipping, and Returns
Original fictional case: Order R81 was physically handed to the carrier on 4 May. The customer was given an estimated arrival date of 6 May. It is now 7 May, and the carrier has not confirmed a new arrival time. Customer Laila needs the item for an event. Agent Tomas can open a carrier inquiry and give a status update at 14:00 today. No replacement dispatch, refund, or revised arrival is authorized in the supplied record. The team must check applicable customer rights and service remedies rather than invent a universal rule.
Language workshop: An expired estimate needs an honest next step
Communication goal: Acknowledge a missed delivery estimate, distinguish shipment from arrival, and explain a concrete follow-up.
Language guidance: A shipping label alone does not prove carrier possession. This case does establish physical handoff. Shipment on 4 May still does not establish delivery on 6 May or any revised arrival time.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): The estimated date has passed, and you still do not have the order. / I understand that you need the item for an event. / The carrier physically received the parcel on 4 May. / I do not have a confirmed revised arrival time. / I can open an inquiry with the carrier now. / I will update you today at 14:00. / That is the update time, not a delivery guarantee. / I will confirm what the tracking record actually shows. / I will remain your contact while we investigate. / No replacement has been authorized in this record. / I will review the applicable service remedies and customer rights. / I need confirmation before giving a new arrival window. / The absence of a new scan does not prove the parcel is lost. / I will record the inquiry against R81. / I will contact you even if the carrier has not answered. / The next message will state the latest position and outstanding action.
Vocabulary: fulfillment: The process of preparing and completing an order's supply. / dispatch: Sending goods from the seller's fulfillment operation. / carrier handoff: Physical transfer of a parcel to the transport provider. / estimated delivery date: A projected arrival date rather than an unconditional guarantee. / tracking event: A recorded milestone in a shipment's movement. / carrier inquiry: A request for the transport provider to investigate a shipment. / proof of delivery: A record supporting that delivery occurred. / delivery exception: An event interrupting the expected delivery process. / last mile: The final transport stage to the recipient. / delivery window: A stated interval during which arrival is expected or scheduled. / service level: The defined standard or terms of a delivery service. / consignment: Goods sent together under a transport arrangement. / tracking number: An identifier used to follow a shipment. / split shipment: An order sent in separate deliveries. / partial fulfillment: Supply of only part of an order. / delivery attempt: An effort to deliver at the specified address. / return to sender: Routing a parcel back to the sending party. / replacement order: An order created to supply a substitute for an earlier item. / refund authorization: Approval to return the relevant payment. / service recovery: Actions addressing a failure in the customer experience. / return authorization: Permission or reference for a return under a process. / reverse logistics: Movement and handling of goods back from customers. / return inspection: Examination of returned goods under the applicable process. / restocking fee: A charge associated with a return where applicable and lawful.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10; distinguish carrier handoff, estimated arrival, and the update time. Swap roles for turns 11-20; offer a review of real remedies without guessing the outcome. Complete the transfer and identify which date is an update rather than an arrival.
Partner: Laila: Customer / Tomas: Customer service agent
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Order V32 was handed to the carrier on 12 June. Its estimated arrival was 14 June. Today is 15 June. A new arrival time is unconfirmed; the agent will update the customer at 16:00.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Laila: Please still call at two if you have not heard back. I need time to arrange something else for the event.
Tomas: I will. The service recovery note will include your concern, the inquiry, and the outstanding answer. You should not have to start the explanation again with another agent.
Laila: Thank you. I need a clear position so I can decide what to do about the event.
Tomas: Understood. We will keep the verified dispatch information separate from arrival and remedy decisions. My next contact is today at two, with the actual position even if some questions remain open.
END REFERENCE

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Professional audience: retail managers, e-commerce teams, merchandising staff, store operations leaders, fulfillment teams, customer service managers, marketplace sellers, and growth operators
Scope: All companies, customers, products, figures, and local procedures are fictional. This book teaches professional English, not legal, accounting, or operational advice. Actual consumer rights, return obligations, marketplace terms, and shipping requirements depend on the transaction and jurisdiction. Store policies do not override applicable rights. Software terminology varies by platform.
Lesson 04: Fulfillment, Shipping, and Returns
Original fictional case: Order R81 was physically handed to the carrier on 4 May. The customer was given an estimated arrival date of 6 May. It is now 7 May, and the carrier has not confirmed a new arrival time. Customer Laila needs the item for an event. Agent Tomas can open a carrier inquiry and give a status update at 14:00 today. No replacement dispatch, refund, or revised arrival is authorized in the supplied record. The team must check applicable customer rights and service remedies rather than invent a universal rule.
Language workshop: An expired estimate needs an honest next step
Communication goal: Acknowledge a missed delivery estimate, distinguish shipment from arrival, and explain a concrete follow-up.
Language guidance: A shipping label alone does not prove carrier possession. This case does establish physical handoff. Shipment on 4 May still does not establish delivery on 6 May or any revised arrival time.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): The estimated date has passed, and you still do not have the order. / I understand that you need the item for an event. / The carrier physically received the parcel on 4 May. / I do not have a confirmed revised arrival time. / I can open an inquiry with the carrier now. / I will update you today at 14:00. / That is the update time, not a delivery guarantee. / I will confirm what the tracking record actually shows. / I will remain your contact while we investigate. / No replacement has been authorized in this record. / I will review the applicable service remedies and customer rights. / I need confirmation before giving a new arrival window. / The absence of a new scan does not prove the parcel is lost. / I will record the inquiry against R81. / I will contact you even if the carrier has not answered. / The next message will state the latest position and outstanding action.
Vocabulary: fulfillment: The process of preparing and completing an order's supply. / dispatch: Sending goods from the seller's fulfillment operation. / carrier handoff: Physical transfer of a parcel to the transport provider. / estimated delivery date: A projected arrival date rather than an unconditional guarantee. / tracking event: A recorded milestone in a shipment's movement. / carrier inquiry: A request for the transport provider to investigate a shipment. / proof of delivery: A record supporting that delivery occurred. / delivery exception: An event interrupting the expected delivery process. / last mile: The final transport stage to the recipient. / delivery window: A stated interval during which arrival is expected or scheduled. / service level: The defined standard or terms of a delivery service. / consignment: Goods sent together under a transport arrangement. / tracking number: An identifier used to follow a shipment. / split shipment: An order sent in separate deliveries. / partial fulfillment: Supply of only part of an order. / delivery attempt: An effort to deliver at the specified address. / return to sender: Routing a parcel back to the sending party. / replacement order: An order created to supply a substitute for an earlier item. / refund authorization: Approval to return the relevant payment. / service recovery: Actions addressing a failure in the customer experience. / return authorization: Permission or reference for a return under a process. / reverse logistics: Movement and handling of goods back from customers. / return inspection: Examination of returned goods under the applicable process. / restocking fee: A charge associated with a return where applicable and lawful.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10; distinguish carrier handoff, estimated arrival, and the update time. Swap roles for turns 11-20; offer a review of real remedies without guessing the outcome. Complete the transfer and identify which date is an update rather than an arrival.
Partner: Laila: Customer / Tomas: Customer service agent
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Order V32 was handed to the carrier on 12 June. Its estimated arrival was 14 June. Today is 15 June. A new arrival time is unconfirmed; the agent will update the customer at 16:00.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Laila: Please still call at two if you have not heard back. I need time to arrange something else for the event.
Tomas: I will. The service recovery note will include your concern, the inquiry, and the outstanding answer. You should not have to start the explanation again with another agent.
Laila: Thank you. I need a clear position so I can decide what to do about the event.
Tomas: Understood. We will keep the verified dispatch information separate from arrival and remedy decisions. My next contact is today at two, with the actual position even if some questions remain open.
END REFERENCE
05Conversion, UX, and Digital AnalyticsA checkout decline with two competing explanations+

Your goal

Report a conversion change accurately and distinguish an observed association from a causal claim.

This lesson in the book, page 40 ↗

A Read the situation

A checkout decline with two competing explanations

Before a checkout redesign, 400 of 1,000 eligible checkout sessions completed a purchase. Afterward, 320 of 1,000 did so. For this report, checkout conversion is completed purchasing sessions divided by eligible checkout sessions; one session counts at most once. A payment-provider outage also occurred in the later period. Event collection and traffic mix have not yet been verified as comparable. Product lead Mara and analyst Dev must explain the decline without claiming that the redesign alone caused it.

Mara: Digital product lead / Dev: Commerce analyst

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What is the later conversion rate?

Answer and explanation

D. 32%

Three hundred twenty completed purchasing sessions divided by one thousand eligible sessions equals thirty-two percent.

2. What is the absolute rate decline?

Answer and explanation

D. Eight percentage points

Forty percent minus thirty-two percent is eight percentage points.

3. Which causal conclusion is established?

Answer and explanation

D. The cause is not isolated by this before-and-after comparison.

The outage and unverified comparability prevent this comparison from isolating one cause.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

The conversion rate fell from 40% to 32%: eight percentage points, or a 20% relative decline from the original rate. These are two descriptions of the same change, not interchangeable units.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
checkout conversion
Completed purchasing sessions divided by the specified checkout-session base.measure checkout conversion
conversion funnel
The ordered steps toward a defined customer action.examine the conversion funnel
checkout abandonment
Failure to complete a purchase after entering checkout.investigate checkout abandonment
eligible session
A session meeting the stated inclusion criteria.define eligible sessions
purchase event
A recorded event representing a purchase action.validate the purchase event
event instrumentation
The implementation of event collection in a digital product.audit event instrumentation
duplicate event
An event recorded more than once for the same action.remove duplicate events
deduplication
Identifying and handling repeated records of the same event.validate deduplication
denominator
The base quantity used in a rate calculation.keep the denominator consistent
percentage point
One unit of difference between percentage rates.report a percentage-point change
relative change
A change expressed as a proportion of its initial value.calculate relative change
traffic mix
The composition of visitors by relevant source or characteristic.compare traffic mix
device segment
A group of users or sessions using a specified device type.analyze a device segment
payment failure
An unsuccessful payment attempt under the relevant process.investigate payment failures
payment gateway
A service connecting a checkout to payment processing.monitor the payment gateway
provider outage
A disruption to a third-party service.isolate the provider outage
confounder
A factor that complicates a causal interpretation.identify a confounder
causal attribution
Assigning an observed outcome to its cause.qualify causal attribution
controlled experiment
A comparison designed to isolate an intervention's effect.run a controlled experiment
random assignment
Allocation by chance to comparison groups.verify random assignment
cohort
A group sharing a defined characteristic or starting event.define a cohort
user experience (UX)
A person's experience of interacting with a product or service.assess the user experience
friction
An obstacle making a customer action harder.reduce checkout friction
rollback
Returning a change to an earlier version.evaluate a rollback

This metric counts sessions completing a purchase, not purchase events or purchased items. A session can contain multiple events. Verify collection, deduplication, eligibility, device mix, and outage exposure before attributing the change.

C Notice the language

Make a challenge testable

A design review can become personal when an unfavorable metric is treated as a verdict on the team. State the measured change, identify competing explanations, and request checks that can distinguish them. Avoid using uncertainty to dismiss a real decline.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
State the observed result
Checkout conversion fell from forty to thirty-two percent.
Use the correct absolute unit
That is a decline of eight percentage points.
Give the relative comparison
Relative to the original rate, the decline is twenty percent.
Define the counted outcome
We count a session once if it completes a purchase.
Protect the denominator
Both periods contain one thousand eligible checkout sessions.
Name the competing event
A payment-provider outage overlapped the later period.
Limit causality
This comparison does not isolate the redesign's effect.
Request a measurement check
Has event collection remained consistent across the release?
Separate events and sessions
More purchase events do not necessarily mean more purchasing sessions.
Check composition
Compare traffic and device mix before interpreting the aggregate.
Investigate the outage
Separate affected and unaffected time intervals.
Avoid a new overclaim
That breakdown would be informative, not automatically causal.
Recognize the business problem
Uncertain attribution does not make the decline unimportant.
Define a next analysis
Validate the data, then compare the relevant segments.
Keep a decision conditional
A rollback decision should use the full risk and performance evidence.
Summarize the finding
We have a measured decline and an unresolved explanation.
6 language and precision notes
Fell from ... to ...
Makes both rates explicit before discussing their difference.
Percentage points
Measures the absolute distance between two percentage rates.
Relative to
Identifies the initial value used for relative change.
Overlapped
Shows co-occurrence without claiming a complete explanation.
Does not isolate
Limits causal inference, not the existence of the observed result.
Not automatically
Prevents a useful diagnostic from becoming an unsupported proof.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which sentence uses the correct units?

Answer and explanation

D. Conversion fell eight percentage points, a twenty-percent relative decline.

The absolute difference is eight points; eight divided by the original forty is twenty percent.

2. Which denominator belongs in this report?

Answer and explanation

D. Eligible checkout sessions

The briefing explicitly defines conversion using the eligible checkout-session base.

3. Which statement overclaims causality?

Answer and explanation

A. The redesign caused the entire decline.

A concurrent outage and unverified comparability leave the redesign's isolated effect unresolved.

4. Which diagnostic is useful but not automatically causal proof?

Answer and explanation

B. Compare outage-affected intervals with unaffected intervals while checking their differences.

Segment comparisons can reveal patterns, but differing populations or timing may still complicate causality.

A checkout decline with two competing explanations

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Mara: Digital product lead / Dev: Commerce analyst

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • causal attribution
  • deduplication
  • percentage points
  • traffic mix
  • event instrumentation
  • rollback
  • denominator
  • device segment

  • checkout conversion
  1. Mara
    Checkout is down after the redesign. The release team wants a rollback recommendation this morning. What can we actually conclude from this chart?
  2. Dev
    The 1 rate did fall, from forty to thirty-two percent. We should acknowledge that result, but the before-and-after chart alone does not identify the cause.
  3. Mara
    I wrote an eight-percent decline in my draft. Is that the clearest description?
  4. Dev
    Use eight 2 for the absolute change. The relative decline is twenty percent because we divide the eight-point difference by the original forty-percent rate.
  5. Mara
    Both periods had one thousand checkout sessions. Does that make the comparison fully controlled?
  6. Dev
    No. A common 3 count does not make the visitors or conditions identical. We still need to verify eligibility, measurement, and the composition of the sessions.
  7. Mara
    The payment provider was down during part of the later period. That seems important.
  8. Dev
    The 4 is a competing explanation we must investigate. We cannot ignore it, but we also cannot assume it accounts for every missing purchase.
  9. Mara
    Can we compare the affected hours with the unaffected hours?
  10. Dev
    Yes, while checking 5. Those intervals may attract different visitors. The breakdown can identify patterns, but it is not automatically a controlled estimate of the redesign's effect.
  11. Mara
    The developers changed some analytics code during the release. Could that affect the result?
  12. Dev
    We should audit 6. Confirm that checkout entry and completion are captured consistently. Otherwise an apparent behavior change may include a measurement change.
  13. Mara
    The purchase-event count is higher than the number of purchasing sessions. Is the dashboard necessarily wrong?
  14. Dev
    Not necessarily. Check 7 and the metric definition. This report counts each eligible purchasing session at most once, not every purchase event or every item purchased.
  15. Mara
    So the same session could produce several recorded events without becoming several successful sessions.
  16. Dev
    Exactly. Then examine each relevant 8. A mobile-specific issue would be useful to locate, but we should not invent one before seeing the breakdown.
  17. Mara
    We still need to make a release decision. Which checks can we finish first, and what would remain uncertain afterward?
  18. Dev
    Agreed. Evaluate a 9 alongside the actual risks and diagnostics. My caution concerns the explanation, not whether the decline deserves urgent attention.
  19. Mara
    I will report the two rates, the outage, and the checks we are doing before claiming a cause.
  20. Dev
    That keeps 10 honest. We have a real reported decline, several testable questions, and a clear next analysis. That is more useful than blaming the release from one chart.

Answers and explanations
  1. checkout conversion Turn 2. Checkout conversion uses completed purchasing sessions over eligible checkout sessions, giving forty percent before and thirty-two percent after.
  2. percentage points Turn 4. Percentage points express the absolute difference between the forty-percent and thirty-two-percent rates.
  3. denominator Turn 6. Denominator is the rate's base; equal base counts do not guarantee equal populations, conditions, or measurement.
  4. provider outage Turn 8. Provider outage names the concurrent service disruption that complicates attributing the whole decline to the redesign.
  5. traffic mix Turn 10. Traffic mix describes the composition of sessions; differences between intervals can complicate interpreting their conversion rates.
  6. event instrumentation Turn 12. Event instrumentation is the collection implementation; a change can alter what is recorded even when behavior is unchanged.
  7. deduplication Turn 14. Deduplication handles repeated records; the session-based definition also requires counting each purchasing session at most once.
  8. device segment Turn 16. Device segment groups sessions by device type, allowing the team to locate patterns hidden by the aggregate rate.
  9. rollback Turn 18. Rollback returns to an earlier version; deciding on it requires the relevant operational risks and evidence, not a fabricated causal certainty.
  10. causal attribution Turn 20. Causal attribution assigns an outcome to a cause; this comparison supports an observed decline but not an isolated redesign effect.

  12. Dev: We should audit event instrumentation. Confirm that checkout entry and completion are captured consistently. Otherwise an apparent behavior change may include a measurement change.
  13. Mara: The purchase-event count is higher than the number of purchasing sessions. Is the dashboard necessarily wrong?
  14. Dev: Not necessarily. Check deduplication and the metric definition. This report counts each eligible purchasing session at most once, not every purchase event or every item purchased.
